<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every action of a countable discrete amenable group on a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite complex C<sup>∗</sup>-algebra that is already Jiang–Su stable absorbs the trivial action on the Jiang–Su algebra up to cocycle conjugacy. No restriction is imposed on the action on the tracial-state simplex. This proves the unital, stably finite case of Szabó's Conjecture A on automatic equivariant Jiang–Su stability.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $A$ be a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite complex $\mathrm C^*$-algebra satisfying $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$. Let $G$ be a countable discrete amenable group and let $\alpha:G\to\operatorname{Aut}(A)$ be any action. Put $$B=A\otimes\mathcal Z,\qquad \beta_g=\alpha_g\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal Z}.$$ There are a unital $*$-isomorphism $\Phi:A\to B$ and unitaries $u_g\in B$, $g\in G$, such that $$\begin{aligned} u_e&=1,& u_{gh}&=u_g\beta_g(u_h),\label{eq:cocycle}\\ \Phi(\alpha_g(a))&=u_g\beta_g(\Phi(a))u_g^* &&(g\in G,\ a\in A).\label{eq:conjugacy} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Every action under the stated algebra and group hypotheses absorbs the trivial Jiang–Su action up to cocycle conjugacy.
- **stmt-thm-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-absorption)): Let a countable discrete amenable group act on a separable, unital, simple, nuclear, $\mathcal Z$-stable $\mathrm C^*$-algebra with nonempty tracial-state space. If the action has equivariant uniform property Gamma, it is cocycle conjugate to its tensor product with the trivial action on $\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: Equivariant uniform property Gamma supplies the absorption implication under the stated tracial hypotheses.
- **stmt-prop-rows** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rows)): There are unital embeddings $\iota_i\colon\mathcal Z\to A^\omega$, $i\geq 1$, for which the unital orbit algebras $$C_i=C^*\bigl(\bar\alpha_g(\iota_i(\mathcal Z)):g\in G\bigr)$$ commute with $A$ and commute pairwise. In particular, $$C=C^*\Bigl(\bigcup_{i\geq 1}C_i\Bigr) \subseteq A^\omega\cap A'$$ is unital, separable, and $\bar\alpha$-invariant, and $\operatorname{T}(C)\neq\varnothing$.* *Moreover, there are self-adjoint elements $x_{i,g}\in C_i$, $i\geq 1$, $g\in G$, and a constant $M<\infty$, such that $$\label{eq:row-normalization} \\|x_{i,g}\\|\leq M,\qquad \varphi(x_{i,g})=0,\qquad \varphi(x_{i,g}^2)=1 \quad\bigl(\varphi\in\operatorname{T}(C)\bigr),$$ and $$\label{eq:row-equivariance} \bar\alpha_h(x_{i,g})=x_{i,hg} \qquad(i\geq1,\ g,h\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Tensorial absorption supplies commuting orbit rows with uniform normalization and exact equivariance.
- **stmt-lem-factor-independence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-factor-independence)): Let $B$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra generated by pairwise commuting unital $C^*$-subalgebras $B_i$, $i\geq1$. If $\varphi\in\partial_{\mathrm e}\operatorname{T}(B)$, then $$\varphi(b_1\cdots b_s)=\prod_{j=1}^s\varphi(b_j)$$ whenever $b_j\in B_{i_j}$ and the indices $i_1,\ldots,i_s$ are distinct.
  - Explanation: Extreme traces factor across distinct commuting rows intrinsically in the generated algebra.
- **stmt-prop-joint-clt** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-joint-clt)): Let $J\subseteq G$ be nonempty and finite. Let $N_j\to\infty$ be a sequence of positive integers, and let $\varphi_j\in\partial_{\mathrm e}\operatorname{T}(C)$. Suppose that $$\label{eq:covariance-convergence} \varphi_j(k_{N_j;g,h})\longrightarrow R_{g,h} \qquad(g,h\in J).$$ Then $R=(R_{g,h})_{g,h\in J}$ is a real positive semidefinite matrix with diagonal entries one. Choose unit vectors $v_g$, $g\in J$, spanning a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space $L$, with $\langle v_g,v_h\rangle=R_{g,h}$. If $W$ is a standard Gaussian vector in $L$ and $\xi_g=\langle v_g,W\rangle$, then $$\bigl((z_{N_j,g})_{g\in J},(k_{N_j;g,h})_{g,h\in J}\bigr) \longrightarrow \bigl((\xi_g)_{g\in J},(R_{g,h})_{g,h\in J}\bigr)$$ in all ordered joint moments under $\varphi_j$. Equivalently, for every noncommutative $*$-polynomial $P$ in these self-adjoint variables, $$\label{eq:joint-polynomial-limit} \varphi_j\!\left( P\bigl((z_{N_j,g})_g,(k_{N_j;g,h})_{g,h}\bigr)\right) \longrightarrow \mathbb E\,P\bigl((\xi_g)_g,(R_{g,h})_{g,h}\bigr).$$ For every sequence $(N_j,\varphi_j)$ as above, a subsequence satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:covariance-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:covariance-convergence">[eq:covariance-convergence]</a> exists.
  - Explanation: Ordered moments of fluctuations and empirical covariances converge along a limiting covariance even as extreme traces vary.
- **stmt-prop-uniform-unitaries** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-unitaries)): For every finite set $H\subset G$, every $\varepsilon>0$, and every integer $m\geq1$, there are $t>0$ and a self-adjoint element $Q\in C$ such that $V=\exp(\mathrm{i}tQ)$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \sup_{\varphi\in\operatorname{T}(C)} \\|\bar\alpha_h(V)-V\\|_{2,\varphi} &<\varepsilon&&(h\in H), \label{eq:uniform-unitary-invariance}\\ \sup_{\varphi\in\operatorname{T}(C)}\|\varphi(V^\ell)\| &<\varepsilon&&(1\leq\ell\leq m). \label{eq:uniform-unitary-moments} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Exponentials of self-adjoint quadratics become approximately invariant with small moments uniformly over all traces.
- **stmt-lem-square-root-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-square-root-trace)): If $S,T$ are positive matrices of the same size, then $$\\|S^{1/2}-T^{1/2}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{\,2}\leq\\|S-T\\|_1.$$
  - Explanation: Trace norm perturbations control the squared Hilbert–Schmidt distance between positive square roots.
- **stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic)): Let $W$ be a standard Gaussian vector in a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space, and let $B$ be a real symmetric operator on that space. Put $$q_B=\langle W,BW\rangle-\operatorname{Tr}(B).$$ Then $$\label{eq:quadratic-variance-characteristic} \\|q_B\\|_{L^2}=\sqrt{2}\,\\|B\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}, \qquad \bigl\|\mathbb E\exp(\mathrm{i}s q_B)\bigr\| \leq\bigl(1+4s^2\\|B\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\bigr)^{-1/4} \quad(s\in\mathbb R).$$ Its law has an exponential moment of its absolute value and is determined by its moments.
  - Explanation: Centered Gaussian quadratics have the stated norm and Fourier bounds and a moment-determinate law.
- **stmt-lem-quadratic-moment-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic-moment-transfer)): Let $X_j=X_j^*$ belong to unital $\mathrm{C}^*$-algebras equipped with states $\varphi_j$. Let $q_B$ be a centered Gaussian quadratic as in Lemma <a href="#lem:gaussian-quadratic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:gaussian-quadratic">9</a>. Suppose that $$\varphi_j(X_j^d)\longrightarrow\mathbb E(q_B^d) \qquad(d=0,1,2,\ldots).$$ Then, for every bounded continuous function $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$, $$\varphi_j(f(X_j))\longrightarrow\mathbb E f(q_B).$$ No uniform bound on $\\|X_j\\|$ is needed.
  - Explanation: Moment convergence to a Gaussian quadratic law implies convergence against bounded continuous functions without a uniform operator norm bound.
- **stmt-lem-coordinate** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-coordinate)): If $x=[(x_j)]\in A^\omega$ has a bounded representative, then $$\begin{aligned} \sup_{\tau\in\operatorname{T}_\omega(A)}\\|x\\|_{2,\tau} &=\lim_{j\to\omega}\sup_{\sigma\in\operatorname{T}(A)}\\|x_j\\|_{2,\sigma}, \label{eq:coordinate-two}\\ \sup_{\tau\in\operatorname{T}_\omega(A)}\|\tau(x)\| &=\lim_{j\to\omega}\sup_{\sigma\in\operatorname{T}(A)}\|\sigma(x_j)\|. \label{eq:coordinate-trace} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, finitely many such tests can be realized simultaneously at a single coordinate with any prescribed positive slack above their ultralimit bounds.
  - Explanation: Finite coordinate tests can be realized simultaneously with positive slack above their ultralimit bounds.
- **stmt-prop-haar** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-haar)): There is a unitary $w\in(A^\omega\cap A')^{\bar\alpha}$ such that, for every $a\in A$, $\tau\in\operatorname{T}_\omega(A)$, and $f\in C(\mathbb T)$, $$\label{eq:haar} \tau(af(w))=\tau(a)\int_{\mathbb T}f\,d\mu,$$ where $\mathbb T=\{z\in\mathbb C:\|z\|=1\}$ and $\mu$ is normalized Haar measure.
  - Explanation: A central invariant unitary has Haar distribution relative to every base element and limit trace.
- **stmt-prop-gamma** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-gamma)): The action $\alpha$ has equivariant uniform property Gamma. The projections in Definition <a href="#def:gamma" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:gamma">2</a> can be chosen to sum to $1$.
  - Explanation: Invariant central projections split limit traces equally and sum to the unit.

In particular, $$C=C^*\\Bigl(\\bigcup_{i\\geq 1}C_i\\Bigr)\n \\subseteq A^\\omega\\cap A'$$ is unital, separable, and $\\bar\\alpha$-invariant, and $\\operatorname{T}(C)\\neq\\varnothing$.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, there are self-adjoint elements $x_{i,g}\\in C_i$, $i\\geq 1$, $g\\in G$, and a constant $M<\\infty$, such that $$\\label{eq:row-normalization}\n \\|x_{i,g}\\|\\leq M,\\qquad\n \\varphi(x_{i,g})=0,\\qquad\n \\varphi(x_{i,g}^2)=1\n \\quad\\bigl(\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)\\bigr),$$ and $$\\label{eq:row-equivariance}\n \\bar\\alpha_h(x_{i,g})=x_{i,hg}\n \\qquad(i\\geq1,\\ g,h\\in G).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are unital embeddings $\\iota_i\\colon\\mathcal Z\\to A^\\omega$, $i\\geq 1$, for which the unital orbit algebras $$C_i=C^*\\bigl(\\bar\\alpha_g(\\iota_i(\\mathcal Z)):g\\in G\\bigr)$$ commute with $A$ and commute pairwise.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-factor-independence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra generated by pairwise commuting unital $C^*$-subalgebras $B_i$, $i\\geq1$.         "source_id": "PM-291.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-joint-clt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J\\subseteq G$ be nonempty and finite. Let $N_j\\to\\infty$ be a sequence of positive integers, and let $\\varphi_j\\in\\partial_{\\mathrm e}\\operatorname{T}(C)$. Suppose that $$\\label{eq:covariance-convergence}\n \\varphi_j(k_{N_j;g,h})\\longrightarrow R_{g,h}\n \\qquad(g,h\\in J).$$ Then $R=(R_{g,h})_{g,h\\in J}$ is a real positive semidefinite matrix with diagonal entries one. Choose unit vectors $v_g$, $g\\in J$, spanning a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space $L$, with $\\langle v_g,v_h\\rangle=R_{g,h}$. If $W$ is a standard Gaussian vector in $L$ and $\\xi_g=\\langle v_g,W\\rangle$, then $$\\bigl((z_{N_j,g})_{g\\in J},(k_{N_j;g,h})_{g,h\\in J}\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\bigl((\\xi_g)_{g\\in J},(R_{g,h})_{g,h\\in J}\\bigr)$$ in all ordered joint moments under $\\varphi_j$. Equivalently, for every noncommutative $*$-polynomial $P$ in these self-adjoint variables, $$\\label{eq:joint-polynomial-limit}\n \\varphi_j\\!\\left(\n P\\bigl((z_{N_j,g})_g,(k_{N_j;g,h})_{g,h}\\bigr)\\right)\n \\longrightarrow\n \\mathbb E\\,P\\bigl((\\xi_g)_g,(R_{g,h})_{g,h}\\bigr).$$ For every sequence $(N_j,\\varphi_j)$ as above, a subsequence satisfying Equation <a href=\"#eq:covariance-convergence\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:covariance-convergence\">[eq:covariance-convergence]</a> exists.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-unitaries",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite set $H\\subset G$, every $\\varepsilon>0$, and every integer $m\\geq1$, there are $t>0$ and a self-adjoint element $Q\\in C$ such that $V=\\exp(\\mathrm{i}tQ)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}\n \\|\\bar\\alpha_h(V)-V\\|_{2,\\varphi}\n &<\\varepsilon&&(h\\in H), \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-invariance}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}|\\varphi(V^\\ell)|\n &<\\varepsilon&&(1\\leq\\ell\\leq m).\n \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-moments}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-uniform-unitaries",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite set $H\\subset G$, every $\\varepsilon>0$, and every integer $m\\geq1$, there are $t>0$ and a self-adjoint element $Q\\in C$ such that $V=\\exp(\\mathrm{i}tQ)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}\n \\|\\bar\\alpha_h(V)-V\\|_{2,\\varphi}\n &<\\varepsilon&&(h\\in H), \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-invariance}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}|\\varphi(V^\\ell)|\n &<\\varepsilon&&(1\\leq\\ell\\leq m).\n \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-moments}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every finite set $H\\subset G$, every $\\varepsilon>0$, and every integer $m\\geq1$, there are $t>0$ and a self-adjoint element $Q\\in C$ such that $V=\\exp(\\mathrm{i}tQ)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}\n \\|\\bar\\alpha_h(V)-V\\|_{2,\\varphi}\n &<\\varepsilon&&(h\\in H), \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-invariance}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\varphi\\in\\operatorname{T}(C)}|\\varphi(V^\\ell)|\n &<\\varepsilon&&(1\\leq\\ell\\leq m).\n \\label{eq:uniform-unitary-moments}\n\\end{aligned}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a standard Gaussian vector in a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space, and let $B$ be a real symmetric operator on that space. Put $$q_B=\\langle W,BW\\rangle-\\operatorname{Tr}(B).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:quadratic-variance-characteristic}\n \\|q_B\\|_{L^2}=\\sqrt{2}\\,\\|B\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}},\n \\qquad\n \\bigl|\\mathbb E\\exp(\\mathrm{i}s q_B)\\bigr|\n \\leq\\bigl(1+4s^2\\|B\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\bigr)^{-1/4}\n \\quad(s\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Its law has an exponential moment of its absolute value and is determined by its moments.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic",

          "source_id": "PM-291.1"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a standard Gaussian vector in a finite-dimensional real Euclidean space, and let $B$ be a real symmetric operator on that space. Put $$q_B=\\langle W,BW\\rangle-\\operatorname{Tr}(B).$$ Then $$\\label{eq:quadratic-variance-characteristic}\n \\|q_B\\|_{L^2}=\\sqrt{2}\\,\\|B\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}},\n \\qquad\n \\bigl|\\mathbb E\\exp(\\mathrm{i}s q_B)\\bigr|\n \\leq\\bigl(1+4s^2\\|B\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2\\bigr)^{-1/4}\n \\quad(s\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Its law has an exponential moment of its absolute value and is determined by its moments.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-moment-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X_j=X_j^*$ belong to unital $\\mathrm{C}^*$-algebras equipped with states $\\varphi_j$. Let $q_B$ be a centered Gaussian quadratic as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:gaussian-quadratic\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:gaussian-quadratic\">9</a>. Suppose that $$\\varphi_j(X_j^d)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb E(q_B^d)\n \\qquad(d=0,1,2,\\ldots).$$ Then, for every bounded continuous function $f:\\mathbb R\\to\\mathbb C$, $$\\varphi_j(f(X_j))\\longrightarrow\\mathbb E f(q_B).$$ No uniform bound on $\\|X_j\\|$ is needed.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-coordinate",
        "exact_quote": "If $x=[(x_j)]\\in A^\\omega$ has a bounded representative, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\tau\\in\\operatorname{T}_\\omega(A)}\\|x\\|_{2,\\tau}\n   &=\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\sup_{\\sigma\\in\\operatorname{T}(A)}\\|x_j\\|_{2,\\sigma},\n       \\label{eq:coordinate-two}\\\\\n \\sup_{\\tau\\in\\operatorname{T}_\\omega(A)}|\\tau(x)|\n   &=\\lim_{j\\to\\omega}\\sup_{\\sigma\\in\\operatorname{T}(A)}|\\sigma(x_j)|.\n       \\label{eq:coordinate-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, finitely many such tests can be realized simultaneously at a single coordinate with any prescribed positive slack above their ultralimit bounds.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Equivariant uniform property Gamma** ([source](#stmt-def-gamma)): The action $\alpha$ has *equivariant uniform property Gamma* if, for every integer $k\geq2$, there are pairwise orthogonal projections $p_1,\ldots,p_k\in(A^\omega\cap A')^{\bar\alpha}$ with $$\label{eq:gamma} \tau(ap_i)=\frac{\tau(a)}{k} \qquad(a\in A,\ \tau\in\operatorname{T}_\omega(A),\ 1\leq i\leq k).$$
- **Tracial-state space** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For a unital $\mathrm C^*$-algebra $D$, write $\operatorname{T}(D)$ for its tracial-state space.
- **Tracial seminorm** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For $\tau\in\operatorname{T}(D)$, set $$
- **Uniform tracial ultrapower** ([source](#eq:ultrapower)): Define $$\label{eq:ultrapower} \\|a\\|_{2,u}=\sup_{\tau\in\operatorname{T}(A)}\\|a\\|_{2,\tau},\qquad A^\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,A)/I_\omega,\qquad I_\omega=\{(a_j):\lim_{j\to\omega}\\|a_j\\|_{2,u}=0\}.$$
- **Centered Gaussian quadratic** ([source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-quadratic)): Put $$q_B=\langle W,BW\rangle-\operatorname{Tr}(B).$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| square-root | \\|S^{1/2}-T^{1/2}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^{\,2} | ≤ | $\\|S-T\\|_1$ | All such pairs. |
| quadratic-norm | \\|q_B\\|_{L^2} | = | $\sqrt{2}\,\\|B\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}$ | All such operators. |
| quadratic-fourier | \bigl\|\mathbb E\exp(\mathrm{i}s q_B)\bigr\| | ≤ | $\bigl(1+4s^2\\|B\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\bigr)^{-1/4}$ | Every real Fourier parameter. |
| sqrt-variance | \operatorname{Var}(q_{P^{1/2}}) | = | $2$ | Every such matrix. |
| raw-variance | \operatorname{Var}(q_P) | = | $2\operatorname{Tr}(P^2)$ | Every such matrix. |
| rank-error | \\|B_E-P_E^{1/2}\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 | ≤ | $\eta^2$ | Polynomial error bounded as specified in the source. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **rows** [claim](#stmt-prop-rows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rows): Relative tail embeddings and induction. -> Commuting invariant orbit rows.
- **independence** [claim](#stmt-lem-factor-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-factor-independence): Tracial factor representation and scalar densities. -> Product formula across distinct rows.
- **moments** [claim](#stmt-prop-joint-clt) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-joint-clt): Row partition expansion and covariance centering. -> Ordered joint Gaussian moment limit.
- **unitaries** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-unitaries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-moment-transfer-2): Square-root polynomial normalization, moment transfer, and compactness of finite covariance matrices. -> Uniform approximately invariant unitaries.
- **haar** [claim](#stmt-prop-haar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-haar): Weighted traces and diagonal coordinate selection. -> Central invariant weighted Haar unitary.
- **gamma** [claim](#stmt-prop-gamma) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gamma): Continuous arc approximation and diagonal coordinate selection. -> Invariant equal trace projections summing to the unit.
- **absorption** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-gamma-2): Absorption criterion and cocycle orientation. -> Cocycle conjugacy with the trivial Jiang–Su tensor action.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-absorption**: The action absorbs the trivial action on the Jiang–Su algebra up to cocycle conjugacy.
- **I-polynomial**: The centered quadratic obtained from the polynomial of the Gram operator equals the covariance-tuple prescription.
- **I-square-root**: Square-root normalization keeps the Gaussian quadratic variance from vanishing as rank grows.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-structural**: Treat arbitrary symmetry-preserving software extensions as cocycle conjugate. -> The required operator algebra and automorphism model has not been established.
- **A-covariance**: Assume the covariance is invariant under translation of its indices. -> The action may move traces, and the Gaussian comparison does not assume covariance invariance.
- **A-finite-certificate**: Promote a finite polynomial test to verification of equivariant absorption. -> The proof also requires moment limits, uniform trace estimates, and diagonal selections in the ultrapower.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The Gram-operator polynomial and its centered covariance-tuple formula are finite constructive expressions. This executable component does not construct the invariant ultrapower unitary or certify cocycle absorption.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases containing unit vectors v, coordinates w, and polynomial coefficients c.
# PRECONDITIONS: Rational entries; nonempty v; common positive dimension; c gives powers starting at one.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact rational centered quadratic values as strings.
# TIME: O(D*d**3 + n*d**2) rational arithmetic operations per case.
# SPACE: O(d**2 + n*d + D) rational entries per case; bit lengths depend on input.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact fmpq rational arithmetic; no floating point or random sampling.
# FAILURE: Invalid dimensions or nonunit vectors raise ValueError; malformed rationals are rejected.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def evaluate(case):
    rows = [[fmpq(x) for x in row] for row in case['v']]
    w = [fmpq(x) for x in case['w']]
    coeffs = [fmpq(x) for x in case['c']]
    n, d = len(rows), len(w)
    if not n or not d or not coeffs or any(len(row) != d for row in rows):
        raise ValueError('dimensions')
    if any(sum(x*x for x in row) != 1 for row in rows):
        raise ValueError('unit vectors required')
    P = [[sum(row[a]*row[b] for row in rows)/n for b in range(d)] for a in range(d)]
    power = [[fmpq(int(a == b)) for b in range(d)] for a in range(d)]
    B = [[fmpq(0) for b in range(d)] for a in range(d)]
    for c in coeffs:
        power = [[sum(power[a][k]*P[k][b] for k in range(d)) for b in range(d)] for a in range(d)]
        B = [[B[a][b] + c*power[a][b] for b in range(d)] for a in range(d)]
    value = sum(w[a]*B[a][b]*w[b] for a in range(d) for b in range(d)) - sum(B[a][a] for a in range(d))
    return str(value)

print(json.dumps([evaluate(case) for case in json.load(sys.stdin)]))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The absorption theorem concerns automorphisms of a Jiang–Su-stable operator algebra; it establishes no correspondence to program transformations or runtime behavior. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gaussian comparison is an ingredient in a proof about tracial operator algebras; the paper specifies no game model or simulation accuracy contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies analytic estimates and existence arguments, but no software tool interface or certified computational workflow. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite covariance polynomial explicitly evaluates a centered Gaussian quadratic from covariance entries, polynomial coefficients, and scalar coordinates. This supplies an adaptable evaluation prescription, while the absorbing isomorphism remains an existence result. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A finite real covariance matrix and its Gram representation organise the Gaussian comparison. Treating these as stored numerical objects is an inferred adaptation; no specialised storage or indexing performance is established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem requires an infinite-dimensional operator algebra and does not specify hardware arithmetic, circuits, or acceleration guarantees. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The stated algebraic absorption result supplies no cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Ordered moment convergence and covariance estimates here have no stated correspondence to a learning objective or generalisation guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Positive covariance matrices support the Gaussian comparison, but the paper states no optimisation objective, feasible-set algorithm, or solver guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The group acts by operator-algebra automorphisms; the paper supplies no correspondence to message-passing processes or protocol correctness. |
| other (Operator algebras and amenable group actions) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The main theorem directly establishes automatic equivariant Jiang–Su stability in the simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite setting, without restrictions on trace orbits. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The absorption theorem requires every stated structural assumption on the algebra and countability, discreteness and amenability of the acting group. Blocked use: ['Applying absorption when any algebraic or group hypothesis is false or unestablished.']
- **L2** (edge_case): Nonunital algebras lie beyond the scope of the main theorem. Blocked use: ['Extending the absorption conclusion to nonunital algebras.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): The trace splittings concern limit traces; these are not identified with all tracial states of the ultrapower. Blocked use: ['Claiming the weighted Haar identity or equal trace splittings for arbitrary ultrapower traces.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): Factor independence across distinct commuting rows is asserted at extreme tracial states. Blocked use: ['Factoring row expectations at arbitrary nonextreme traces without another argument.']
- **L5** (approximation): The joint limit is convergence of ordered moments along diverging row counts with convergent empirical covariance; it is not an exact finite-row Gaussian law. Blocked use: ['Replacing finite-row moments by their Gaussian limits as exact identities.']
- **L6** (nonconstructive): Uniform approximate unitary estimates are proved by a subsequence contradiction; the argument supplies no explicit uniform finite-row convergence rate. Blocked use: ['Using the proof as a certified finite stopping rule for the approximation.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The construction fixes the polynomial and its parameters before increasing the number of rows, independently of traces and limiting covariance. Blocked use: ['Changing the polynomial using a selected limiting covariance while invoking the same uniformity argument.']
- **L8** (formal_scope): The empirical covariance array is used as an ordered noncommutative polynomial; positivity is not required of this array. Blocked use: ['Applying matrix square-root functional calculus directly to the empirical covariance entries.']
- **L9** (edge_case): The Gaussian comparison permits singular covariance matrices and does not assume translation invariance of covariance. Blocked use: ['Rejecting singular covariance as outside the Gaussian comparison.', 'Imposing covariance invariance as a necessary hypothesis.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The moment-transfer conclusion concerns bounded continuous functions and assumes convergence of every scalar moment to a centered Gaussian quadratic law. Blocked use: ['Inferring operator-norm convergence.', 'Transferring arbitrary unbounded functions from finitely many checked moments.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The invariant central Haar unitary and equal trace projections are obtained as ultrapower classes through diagonal selections. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite representative as an exactly central invariant unitary or projection in the base algebra.']
- **L12** (unknown): A direct correspondence to software components, physical devices or engineering performance specifications is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the absorption theorem directly certifies software behavior or physical performance.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every action of a countable discrete amenable group on a simple, separable, unital, infinite-dimensional, nuclear, stably finite complex C<sup>∗</sup>-algebra that is already Jiang–Su stable absorbs the trivial action on the Jiang–Su algebra up to cocycle conjugacy. No restriction is imposed on the action on the tracial-state simplex. This proves the unital, stably finite case of Szabó's Conjecture A on automatic equivariant Jiang–Su stability.
